THE CLOCK: one registered `@property --v3-t` (0 = 11 Aug, 1 = 27 Aug) drives every colour on the page; no section writes a colour, a section that wants another time of day writes `--v3-t`. Three paths, mutually exclusive: (1) pure CSS `animation-timeline: scroll(root block)` on `.v3`; (2) rAF fallback in tick-bar.tsx, which stamps data-ramp="raf" and writes `--v3-t` inline, and runs ONLY where CSS.supports('animation-timeline','scroll()') is false; (3) three fixed bands from [data-stage] for no-JS + no-scroll-timeline, and for reduced motion. Ramp derivation is declared on `.v3, .v3-bar, .v3-band` (not once at the root) because a custom property's var() refs are substituted where declared - that is what lets a band override the clock and get its own colours. RAMP SHAPE, read this before building sections 2-10: an inverting page has a mid-tone band where NEITHER light nor dark ink reaches 4.5:1 (light fails above bg luminance 0.156, dark below 0.210). So the ground spends 52% of scroll on night->blue hour, 0.4% crossing dawn, 47% on first light->day, and the ink turns over inside that 0.4% at the point where both read ~4:1. Measured in Chrome at the hero: t=0 16.63:1, 0.25 13.73, 0.50 10.29, 0.520 10.01, 0.5225 4.90, 0.5230 4.28, 0.5232 4.12 (worst, the turn), 0.5240 4.69, 0.53 4.96, 0.56 5.42, 0.62 6.41, 0.80 10.21, 1.0 16.07. Sub-AA window is t in [0.5228, 0.5238] = 0.1% of scroll, a transit no section rests in. CONSEQUENCE: body text on this page is always full --v3-fg. There is no muted body token; a 72% ink on the first-light ground is 3.3:1. Separate text by size and rule, never by opacity. TICK BAR: 48px sticky, the only sticky element, nothing pinned, no scroll hijack. Marker position, lit ticks, date readout and tick entrance are ALL CSS off --v3-t - no JS in the bar except the rAF fallback, so the readout is right with scripting off and can never disagree with the marker. Sixteen dates stacked in one tabular box, nearest-tick wins over a half-open window (a symmetric window shows nothing when the clock lands on an exact half, which it does on a short page - fixed). Ticks draw once, 640ms (15 x 25ms + 265ms). Hover/focus on a tick shows that day's event under the bar via position:fixed at 48px (the bar is sticky at top 0 and first in the document, so fixed is always exactly under it). DATES: sixteen ticks have to cover seventeen calendar dates, so 18 Aug gets no tick - it is the only dropped date that carries no sourced event, and dropping it keeps 11, 12, 19, 26 and 27 Aug each on their own tick. Five events, all from brief 4.1, on ticks 1/2/8/15/16; the other eleven ticks show nothing on hover, by design. TICK -> SECTION: tick 1 hero, 2 services, 3-14 work, 15 process, 16 proof (the flash-forward = 27 Aug = the launch). Sections carry `built:false` today so ticks 2-16 render as spans, not dead links; they become links automatically as sections land. LCP is the headline; hero.tsx is a server component and the only client code in it is the ring. RING: mounts only >=1100px, double-gated (CSS media query reserves+hides the 200px column, useMinWidth decides whether <Scene> mounts). Camera is a deliberate long lens (4deg at 56 units) because an SVG arc cannot do perspective - at a normal focal length the canvas sat ~6px above the SVG behind it and read as a doubled stroke. SVG fallback is ~3KB, same 16-segment logic driven by the same --v3-t in pure CSS, complete under reduced motion. fps=8 because the scene is static between tick changes and <Scene> owns the frame driver (no frameloop=demand exposed). THE ONE `!important` ON THIS PAGE, and it is answering another one. All seven answers are in the SSR HTML with the `hidden` attribute (curl of /v3: 7/7 questions, 7 `hidden` panels, 7 aria-expanded=false, full answer text present), but Tailwind preflight ships `[hidden]:where(:not([hidden=until-found])){display:none!important}` and a display:none box cannot transition. Entry 08 restyles the closed state instead: `visibility:hidden` (out of the a11y tree - checkVisibility({visibilityProperty:true}) false - out of find-in-page, out of the tab order, nothing painted) plus a 0fr row, and only the `display` override sits in `@layer base`, because an important declaration in a layer beats an important declaration in no layer whatever the specificity. Only display is layered; the three normal declarations stay unlayered or they would lose to the open state. Also a `<noscript>` rule that stands all seven answers open with scripting off, so the buttons are never dead.
